TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL
incl. fall-through cost		 370368 ms vs Baseline 689499 ms

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 70133 ms + 3072 ms + 92072 ms = 165277 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 165277 ms + 205090 ms (61 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 370368 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 689499 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack fetch+decode — pack download + stream-decode time, charged to this asset only on the FIRST hit per pack (subsequent hits show 0 ms · cached).
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL
incl. fall-through cost		 86174 ms vs Baseline 99555 ms

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 10020 ms + 995 ms + 24214 ms = 35228 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 35228 ms + 50946 ms (59 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 86174 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 99555 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack fetch+decode — pack download + stream-decode time, charged to this asset only on the FIRST hit per pack (subsequent hits show 0 ms · cached).
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

column sums		 16217 ms
15 pack fetches paid
· rest cached		 940 ms 24739 ms 41896 ms 689499 ms
158 asset fetches		 14.92 MB
TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL
incl. fall-through cost		 246694 ms vs Baseline 689499 ms

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 16217 ms + 940 ms + 24739 ms = 41896 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 41896 ms + 204798 ms (58 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 246694 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 689499 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack fetch+decode — pack download + stream-decode time, charged to this asset only on the FIRST hit per pack (subsequent hits show 0 ms · cached).
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL
incl. fall-through cost		 81490 ms vs Baseline 99555 ms

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 11304 ms + 1239 ms + 17930 ms = 30474 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 30474 ms + 51016 ms (62 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 81490 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 99555 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack fetch+decode — pack download + stream-decode time, charged to this asset only on the FIRST hit per pack (subsequent hits show 0 ms · cached).
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL
incl. fall-through cost		 233899 ms vs Baseline 689499 ms

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 9406 ms + 948 ms + 18455 ms = 28809 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 28809 ms + 205090 ms (61 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 233899 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 689499 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack fetch+decode — pack download + stream-decode time, charged to this asset only on the FIRST hit per pack (subsequent hits show 0 ms · cached).
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL
incl. fall-through cost		 270657 ms vs Baseline 99555 ms

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 103686 ms + 7327 ms + 108697 ms = 219710 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 219710 ms + 50947 ms (60 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 270657 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 99555 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack fetch+decode — pack download + stream-decode time, charged to this asset only on the FIRST hit per pack (subsequent hits show 0 ms · cached).
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

column sums		 28435 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL
incl. fall-through cost		 251543 ms vs Baseline 689499 ms

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 28435 ms + 112 ms + 18170 ms = 46717 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 46717 ms + 204826 ms (59 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 251543 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 689499 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack fetch+decode — pack download + stream-decode time, charged to this asset only on the FIRST hit per pack (subsequent hits show 0 ms · cached).
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

column sums		 21205 ms
93 pack fetches paid
· rest cached		 103 ms 23626 ms 44934 ms 99555 ms
162 asset fetches		 321.8 KB
TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL
incl. fall-through cost		 67613 ms vs Baseline 99555 ms

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 21205 ms + 103 ms + 23626 ms = 44934 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 44934 ms + 22679 ms (53 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 67613 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 99555 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack fetch+decode — pack download + stream-decode time, charged to this asset only on the FIRST hit per pack (subsequent hits show 0 ms · cached).
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Legend
  • Baseline — no interception. Browser fetches assets natively. Closest to real production traffic.
  • Baseline-CDP — same harness rig as TP (intercept + fulfill via CDP) but bytes refetched Node-side. Lets us isolate harness IPC tax. Network behaviour differs from baseline (no H/2 multiplexing on Node fetches).
  • TokenPack — intercept + serve from packed PNG. Pays harness IPC tax on every served asset.
  • Δ TP vs BL (prod fairness) — TP minus baseline. Real comparison including harness handicap. If TP wins here, it wins despite the rig.
  • Δ TP vs BL-CDP (tech fairness) — TP minus baseline-CDP. Both pay harness tax, so this isolates pack-vs-direct. Tech-level win.
  • Est. prod TP (–harness) — projection of TP duration with harness IPC tax stripped out. Computed as TP × (1 − harnessRatio) where harnessRatio = sum(harness)/sum(totalMs) across all served-from-pack assets. Approximates a real Service-Worker deployment where the browser delivers pack bytes directly without CDP roundtrips. Estimate, not measurement.
